# Plan DAG

This file lists the remaining work on Zed and the order it must run in. It has 553 items, 2078 edges and 80 levels (0 to 79). It covers the TODO items, the store fixes, the left-recursion work, the T14 and T11 findings and every unchecked step in `../zed/PROGRESS.md`.

**Needs relaxed on 2026-11-02.** Items now run in parallel in their own worktrees, and a merge resolves a small overlap in a file. So the `needs` column keeps only three kinds of edge: code (one item uses or changes code the other writes), measure (one item measures code the other changes) and files-heavy (both rewrite the same functions). Edges of the other kinds were dropped: files-light (the same file, different functions), order (a preferred order with no technical cause), docs (one item only reads the other's text) and none (no reason found). Of the 1134 edges classified, 907 were code, 109 measure, 13 files-heavy, 52 files-light, 35 order, 3 docs and 15 none. Where the reviewer of the classification disagreed, its judgment was used. T11-fix was split into T11-fix-store and T11-fix-rest, X14 was added, and edges found missing were added after reading the cited code.

## How to read it

- An edge means one item needs another. The item in the first column cannot start until every id in its `needs` column is merged.
- The level of an item is the length of the longest path from a root, where a root is an item with no needs. Level 0 holds the roots.
- Two items with no directed path between them, in either direction, may run in parallel. Two items at the same level never have a path between them.
- Levels are a convenience. Items at different levels can also be unordered, and may then run in parallel. Use the `needs` column, not the level number, to decide.
- A file conflict is one item's path equal to, or inside a directory of, the other's. Since 2026-11-02 a file conflict between two items that the DAG does not order no longer forces an order: both may run, and the merge resolves the overlap. Run them apart where possible. The conflict lists compare paths as written, so they do not show a conflict through a glob, such as `tests/steps/*/scaling.rs` (T2 and T12).
- An edge is added only when the code, a measurement or a rewrite of the same functions requires it. A missing edge of that kind costs a bad merge; an edge for a light file overlap costs parallel time and is left out.
- Reasons for the edges are in [Why each item waits](#why-each-item-waits). Where a reason or note disagrees with the `needs` column, the `needs` column wins, since the notes were written before the corrections below.

**Done, so not listed:** T1 (all crates optimized in dev builds, `Cargo.toml` `[profile.dev]` at `opt-level = 2`) and T8 (code in other languages removed). Both landed in commit cd2d30b. Every step L1.1 to L1.39 and M0.x is also done, so a need on one of them is treated as met and left out of the table. The exceptions are L1.17a, L1.29a, L1.37a, L1.39a and L1.39b, added on 2026-11-03, which have rows.

**Files every step touches:** `tests/steps/main.rs`, `tests/steps/m0_01_scaffolding/`, `STATUS.md`, `COMPLEXITY.md` and `../zed/PROGRESS.md`. These are left out of the conflict lists, since every pair would match. The changes are additive, so resolve them at merge by keeping both sides, then regenerate `PROGRESS.md`. Each step's own `tests/steps/lNN_MM_*/` directory is separate from every other step's.

## Corrections to the supplied graph

The levels and conflict list that the planning script produced were wrong, and were discarded and recomputed here.

- **Reversed edges (confirmed).** The critic's edges use the convention "from needs to", as its first edge reason states. The script applied them as "to needs from". All 23 added edges were reversed, and none of the 22 dropped edges took effect. The result held one strongly connected component of 133 items and one of 3 (T11-fix, T12, T14-lints), and its levels had no meaning (L1.40 at 2, T2-mid at 71).
- **Fix.** The reversed edges were removed, the additions were applied in the stated direction, and the drops were applied. Items T9, X-round2 and LR do not exist, so needs on them were removed: T9 is the same work as T4, X-round2 is replaced by T6 (which needs XE2), and LR is replaced by LR2. Reason texts were updated to match. The recomputed graph has no cycle, and each item's level is one more than the highest level among its needs. Both were checked by script against the table in this file.
- **T11-S22 level (2026-11-03).** T11-S22 was listed at level 1 though it needs T11-preimage, also at level 1. It is at level 2.
- **S22b-rest level (2026-11-03).** S22b-rest was listed at level 2 though it needs S22b, also at level 2. It is at level 3. Its row had also been left out of the header counts.
- **Needs with no row (2026-11-03).** TD-values needed TRADEOFF-DOC, T6-ratlit needed CAP-bits and T2-mid needed ST-deep-stack, and none of the three had a row. All three are done and merged (ledger and commits named in their rows), so each has a Done row at level 0. No other level changes. `cargo run -q -p zed-tools --bin plan` now checks that every need has a row, that no id has two rows, that each level is one more than the highest level among its needs, and the counts in the first paragraph.
- **S22e-quad level (2026-11-03).** S22e-quad was listed at level 2 though it needs S22e, also at level 2. It is at level 3. No item needs it.
- **Plan audit (2026-11-03).** 73 rows were added: one for each step added to DESIGN.md by the audit (L1.29a to L33.3a), and one for each item that had ledger lines but no row and is not done. Each new row feeds the item its ledger line names, or the end-of-layer test of its layer. A need on an item that is done and has no row was left out (CORPUS-parse, TYPE-forms, JIT-reloc, T6-payload-pinwheel, PLAN-hygiene, XR3-synth, S22-merge, LR4 and the done L1 steps). Needs added to existing rows: L3.8 needs L2.14, L23.6 needs L5.14, L34.8 needs L34.2, L20.5 needs L20.3a, T14-rest needs T14. L2.11 does not need L7.3, since L7.3 already depends on L2.11 through L2.14; `below(n)`, which reads the random-bits port, is step L10.3a, which needs both. Each end-of-layer test from L8.3 to L35.2 needs S.6 and reruns it, as L7.15 already did. XS22 is done (its synthesis is `synth/SYNTH_R1.md`, built as S22a). The titles of L2.4, L6.3, L6.7, L6.14, L7.3, L7.4 and L7.15 now name the work DESIGN.md gives those steps. Q1-LOCAL is deferred and feeds nothing. HKT-1 keeps its id: the number is the HKT study's item label, not an order. S22-more-random-fail found nothing to repair. The file-conflict tables below were not regenerated; that is item PLAN-conflicts.
- **DAG audit (2026-11-03).** The graph that dagview builds (the union of these rows, the ledger and the overrides) had one cycle, T6-kinds and KINDS-reject: the plan audit's ledger line had KINDS-reject feed T6-kinds, and a later line (16:37) has it need T6-kinds, since the rejection holds only until T6-kinds builds the forms. The later line holds: T6-kinds no longer needs KINDS-reject, KINDS-reject needs T6-kinds, and an override drops the old ledger edge. 65 rows were added: each ledger item that is not done and had no row, and each done item that T12 or DAG-audit needs, as a Done row. Each row's needs now equal the needs dagview shows, limited to ids with a row, so the two agree; a need on a done item with no row is still left out. T12 needs every item that writes Rust and comes before L1.41 without coming after T12, done items included, so that its check covers their code (186 needs); the five items whose place was open (L1.40, L1.40a, L1.40b, L1.40c, S22-R2) write Rust and come before L1.41, so T12 needs them. Every end-of-layer test from L7.15 to L35.2 (the rows ending "S.6 rerun") needs T12, and its title says the scaling-coverage check passes. BUGCLASS-hunt and T11-S22 are done and their rows say so. T11-ty's title held `|` characters that split its cells; it now reads "O(size of `Ty`)". Five items are not steps of Zed and are out of the graph (user, 2026-11-03): DAGVIEW and DAGVIEW-elk (the plan viewer), PLAN-RELAX (an edit to this file, 4420380), TIMING and CONTROLS (test-harness work). TIMING's row is removed; each of the five has an override `outside`, so dagview draws no box for it and no edge names it, and its ledger lines stay as history. P14-MST, the O(n log n) reference program 14, had no item after it: L7.13 now needs it, since L7.13 runs corpus program 14 (sec:plan, the corpus by layer: L7 has 14), and each corpus program's expected output is what its reference program prints (M0.4; `tests/steps/m0_04_corpus_references/mod.rs`), so the reference fixes the output program 14 is checked against at every size; XP14 reaches L7.13 through P14-MST. The table now has 513 items, 1927 edges and 77 levels. The file-conflict tables were not regenerated (item PLAN-conflicts).

## File conflicts across levels

These 1369 pairs, the 91 in the four lines added on 2026-11-03 for L1.40a, L1.40b, L1.40c and L12.3a, the 150 in the five lines added on 2026-11-03 for GLAM-diamond, SPECULATE, GLAM-logspec, GLAM-diamond-iface and GLAM-pin, and the 60 in the last two lines (added on 2026-11-03 for HKT-2 and HKT-1; the pair HKT-2, HKT-1 is in both lines), none of these counts including files under `tests/`, are at different levels, have no directed path between them, and share a file, so the same rule applies: they may run in parallel, and the merge resolves the overlap. Pairs that became unordered only because an edge was dropped on 2026-11-02 are not listed; the overlaps among them that matter are named in [Why each item waits](#why-each-item-waits). Each line gives an item and the items it conflicts with in this way. ## LR4: associativity audit

LR4 (after LR2; LR3 was stopped by the author) audited every rule of sec:grammar for the
direction it nests in; the table is `docs/grammar_assoc.md`. It converted `Expr12` (`else`
chains) and the `&&` join chain (`AndJoin`) to left recursion and kept every other rule. It
touches `crates/zedc/src/ast/mod.rs` (arm `"Expr12"`), which L2.1 also edits (`reduce_tail`
near the same match): a merge of the two needs a look at that match, nothing more. DESIGN.md
changes in sec:grammar (PEG and desugar) and in the sec:joins Planning bullet.

## Machine limits and tools

The author installed the tools below on 2026-11-02. Each was run that day on a small input, and the versions are the ones reported. All tests stay under 60 s each and 112 GB in total. There are two test suites. The local suite, `tools/run_tests.sh`, needs only the Rust toolchain and the cores of the machine, and it is what CI runs: tests in `tests/steps/`. The full battery, `tools/run_tests.sh --full`, is the local suite plus `tests/full/<step>/`, each with a file `needs` listing its tools. It stops before any test and names what is missing, so nothing is skipped. `tools/fetch_test_deps.sh` fetches into `~/.cache/zed-test-deps`, with no root, what Fedora does not package: target sysroots, the official rustc for target builds, and Podman's test storage and image. Every test that uses a tool in this table goes in `tests/full/`, and a step with such tests runs `--full` before it merges. Plain `cargo test` does not build `tests/full/`; clippy in CI compiles it.

| Items | Tools and what was run | What to do |
|---|---|---|
| L21.1, L21.3 | PostgreSQL 18.6 (`initdb`, `pg_ctl`, a query), MariaDB 11.8.8 (`mariadb-install-db`, `mariadbd` as `mysqld`, a query), SQLite 3.51.2. | Run private instances as the user, in a temporary directory, on a socket with networking off. Do not use the system services. |
| L26.1 | `wasmtime` 50.0.1 from cargo, in `~/.cargo/bin`, which must be on `PATH`. Ran a module built with `clang --target=wasm32` and `wasm-ld`. | Use `wasmtime` for the cross-target tests. |
| L27.1 | RTX 3050, driver 615.78.08, Vulkan 1.4. `llvmpipe` software Vulkan is also present. | Run the GPU tests on the card locally. `llvmpipe` gives a software device where no GPU exists. |
| L27.2 | Multi-host behavior runs on one machine. Rootless Podman 5.8.9 with `crun` 1.28: two containers on a private network reached each other, and `podman kill` (0.1 s), `pause`, and `network disconnect` and `connect` worked. A paused container's processes stop, but its kernel still answers ping, so a pause test must watch the protocol, not ping. A container starts in 1.5 s. A veth pair between user network namespaces with `tc netem` delayed packets, which needs no root. KVM (`/dev/kvm`, `qemu-system-x86_64`) is also present. | Each node is a rootless Podman container, with the storage from `tools/fetch_test_deps.sh`. Stopping a machine is `podman kill`, pausing one past its lease is `podman pause`, a partition is `podman network disconnect`. Delay and loss use `netem` on a veth pair. Use a KVM VM only for what a container cannot show, such as a kernel crash. Do not use Docker. |
| L27.3 | Yosys 0.67 (synthesis of a small module), Verilator 5.046 (lint), Icarus Verilog 13.0 (simulation). | Use these. |
| L28.1, L28.2 | `mlir-opt`, `mlir-translate`, `llc`, `opt` and `wasm-ld`, all LLVM 22.1.8, matching `clang`. Lowered a small module from MLIR through `llc`. | Use these. |
| L29.1, L29.2 | `qemu-aarch64` and `qemu-riscv64` 10.2.2, `clang`, `lld`, and gdb 18.1, which reads both targets. With the sysroots from `tools/fetch_test_deps.sh`, C, C++, static C and Rust programs ran under qemu on both targets, gdb stopped at a breakpoint through `qemu -g`, and `zed-rt` built for aarch64 with the official rustc 1.99.1. | Link with `~/.cache/zed-test-deps/bin/<arch>-cc`, run with `qemu-<arch> -L ~/.cache/zed-test-deps/<arch>/usr/<arch>-linux-gnu`, and build target Rust with `~/.cache/zed-test-deps/rust/bin/rustc`. Fedora's rustc rejects the official target std with E0514. `tests/full/s00_01_tools/` shows each command. |
| L30.1, L30.6 | `code`, node v22, `Xvfb` and `xvfb-run` (ran a command under `xvfb-run`). | Run headless editor tests under `xvfb-run -a`. |
| L5.14, L23.5 | Timing runs must not share the 32 cores with other branches. | Run these alone, with no parallel branch active. |
| L7.15 | The 10 GB demo must fit the 112 GB and 60 s caps. | Size the run to fit, and report the size used. |
| L19.3 | The run takes over 60 s. | Split it per program. |
| XE2 | Codex and `agy` logins; `agy`'s three environment variables. | Running now. If a login fails, report it. |

CI has none of these tools and runs only the local suite, so each item in this table puts the tests that use them in `tests/full/`. Timing tests can fail when other work loads the machine (5 pieces failed at load 57 on 32 cores and passed when rerun alone), so read a failure under load by rerunning the piece before calling it a regression.

## Why each item waits

- **T2-eval.** No needs. *Note:* on_stack is called at product.rs:1281,1508,1637 and preimage.rs:170,2204,2225,2294, so removing it touches product.rs; do that part after ST1 or keep on_stack as a thin no-op until T2-mid deletes it. Keeping the no-op wrapper is the recommended way to avoid an edge to ST1 and ST2. *Note:* MAX_DEPTH was the hidden limit set by the thread stack. It is gone: DESIGN.md sec:kernel clause (i) now has no nesting limit, and the operations on `Value` and `Family` hold their depth on the heap (repair T2-eval-repair).
- **T3.** No needs. *Note:* Must precede L1.40 by user order (item 3); no technical tie. Stale TODO: Z8 is marked fixed at COMPLEXITY.md:111 and tables.rs has port_at; the stopped patch holds const_at and lambda_at. Confirm const_at and lambda_at are in tables.rs before starting (the row says so; I read only the port_at part). What is really open is Z9: visit clones each node at tables.rs:106,109, and NodeInfo owns node and alone: Region. The cheaper fix may be a borrowed Tables<'a> or Arc-shared regions in zir, not imbl; imbl is not a zed-native dependency yet. The patch also holds an unrelated `early` condition release for if in codegen.rs; check whether that landed before deleting it.
- **T4.** No needs. *Note:* Must precede L1.40 technically as well as by user order (items 4, 9): HC4 narrowing runs through Scope::reduce and project into this solver, and L1.40's scaling numbers would measure the cubic cost otherwise. Edits outside presburger.rs are the callers of a new refusal error: today callers use Over (product.rs:1201,1238, preimage.rs:267-289, hull.rs), and a refusal needs a variant and a diagnostic row, plus DESIGN text for that error. So T4 goes before ST1 and ST2 on product.rs. Cross-review (no-thresholds, hard-algorithms rules) is needed only if no bound is found without it; if it is needed, add an edge from XE2 or a separate round. Fix the stale comment at scaling.rs:93 here, since T10 would otherwise edit the same file.
- **ST3.** No needs. *Note:* Spec-repo paragraph only, at DESIGN.md:1673 or the emptiness text it names. Do before XE2 so the brief reads it. Regenerate PROGRESS.md after a plan-step edit. Listed among the open L1.39 items, so before L1.40, though only by that listing.
- **T11.** No needs. *Note:* Part of the T14 review stream; reads the central tree and edits nothing, so can run alongside T13 and T14 at once. Size of the scan: 1,019 .clone() calls, and Arc or Rc on 343 lines (zedc 752 and 227, zed-kernel 219 and 96, zed-native 28 and 10, zed-rt 20 and 10). Not needed by L1.40. Findings in product.rs, tables.rs and bdd.rs overlap ST1, T3 and T6: drop those rather than fix them twice. Done at f425b32; the nodes are named in [T14 and T11 findings](#t14-and-t11-findings).
- **T13.** No needs. *Note:* Marked EARLY by the user. Writes one new file and reads the rest, so it conflicts with nothing. File:line examples will go stale as T2, T11-fix-store and T11-fix-rest rewrite code; cite by function name as well. Not needed by L1.40. From then on, every step adds its gaps when complete.
- **T14.** No needs. *Note:* Read only; each fix becomes its own DAG node with edges to any forward branch on the same files. T14 itself blocks nothing: no item needs it, and it does not block L1.40 or any step. It ended at f425b32 with its coverage incomplete, so its findings are in the nodes named in [T14 and T11 findings](#t14-and-t11-findings), and T14-rest reviews the rest. T11 and T13 are part of this stream.
- **LR1.** No needs. *Note:* Algorithm is in ../squirrelparser/paper/squirrel_parser.tex fig. Code: memo entry with inRecPath, foundLeftRec and a per-position version; expansion loop; never replace a match by a mismatch. Zed specifics: memo key is (rule, pos, head), so the version per position must cover both head values; `expected` and `far` keep the failures of the last failed expansion, since that pass reaches the operator a flat `*` loop stops at and dropping them would lose operators from the messages (the aim, unchanged tests/steps/l01_08_parser/errors.rs, holds; see STATUS.md); a re-match leaves dead nodes in the arena (linear, acceptable). Paper's quadratic case: lookahead over a left-recursive rule at every position. The grammar's lookaheads `&('{' Item)` at syntax.peg:138 and `!(Name ':') Expr` at :123 need a scaling test after LR2. Must precede LR2, T2-parse and L2.1. Shares no file with L1.40, so can run alongside it.
- **T14-bugs-rt.** No needs. *Note:* Confirmed by T14: the term shaped `let q = (((1, "x")), 1+1)`, matched on (q, q) with a binder under the constant, gives the value in the kernel and in native with fuse=false, and `runtime: a word tagged 4 has no parts` with fuse=true. `cell_at` in zir/memory.rs treats the same case as None. Fixed in commit c5362b5, with its test. The ledger records this repair under the id T14.
- **T2-native.** T3: rewrites visit and NodeInfo in the same file *Note:* Could be folded into T3 as one change. Per COMPLEXITY.md Z9, n nested ifs overflowed the stack in code generation and in the tables; Z9b is fixed (COMPLEXITY.md). T3 (d5e160e) landed the walks of Tables::visit and the code generator. Left here: `Heap::fresh` and `Heap::value` recurse on value depth, and no other T2 item names zed-rt, so a long cons list can overflow the stack (T14, read from the code, not run). Fix: manual stacks.
- **XE2.** No needs. *Note:* Edits no repo files during the review; reports go to the scratchpad, and each round is committed per the cross-review rules. Needs external Codex and agy logins and agy's three environment variables. Brief must carry the rejected designs and scaling rules verbatim, and say no Python. Not technically needed by L1.40. Blocks T5, T6 and ST1. Round-1 reports may be gone from the scratchpad; the memory file has their summary.
- **X14.** XE2: the cross-review xs builds on the xe round-3 synthesis. *Note:* Added 2026-11-02. One large tuple SCC in type inhabitation (the delcheck worklist) stays quadratic; round 2 looks for a better algorithm (brief in wt/xs/BRIEF1.md). It edits no repo file; T6 builds the result.
- **XT3.** No needs. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 by XT-R, which built design R of the xt round-2 synthesis (`wt/xt2/synth/SYNTH_R2.md`): box stages in place of the simplex, the column quotient and the UTVPI potential on a DAG. Open from it: cyclic UTVPI rows still take O(V·E) by Bellman–Ford–Moore (the cyclic hub, 64 → 512, measured 42.5 times the work); without the linear program design R refuses questions it used to decide (option A, STATUS.md, L1.38: the small-coefficient oracle 229/21/150 → 213/3/184, and one projection of 60 in `random.rs` wider than its hull); a regular 8-variable system with a point is refused under every naming, but whether a refusal holds can depend on the name tie-break in `canonical()`; and three-variable rows tight at a point are refused (`three_variables_in_many_rows_scale`). It edits no repo file; a later implementation item builds the result.
- **UTVPI-GOR.** XT3: B1 is its method and probe. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03. Feeds UTVPI-NEAR and L1.40.
- **BOX-5pt.** XT3: B3 is its method and probe. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03. Feeds HULL3, XR3 and L1.40.

Decides every system HULL3 decides, so HULL3 is subsumed in what it decides (ledger item HULL3-subsumed); also covers what HULL3-GEN was for.
- **ST2.** T4: edits product.rs project_part and its error paths, and re-projecting more often multiplies the solver's cost *Note:* Must precede L1.40 technically: reaching the fixed point decides what HC4 narrowing sees, so L1.40's arm-type tests depend on it. Both are pre-existing flaws. Fix for reduce loop: compare against the names after project ran only if project changed nothing; with a termination test (widening or finite interval pieces). project_due is a stated precision trade for linearity; fixing it must not make the cost above O(n log n): key by the context's version or a hash of the constraints used. If no such key holds the bound, record the imprecision in DESIGN L1.39 and name it there.
- **LR2.** LR1: left-recursive rules do not parse without it *Note:* Must precede L2.1 and every later step that adds AST forms (L2.2, L2.4, L22.1, and so on), since each such step would otherwise be written twice. L1.40 does not need it: its test has no new syntax. L1.41 does not need it if ast::Expr is unchanged, so ZIR stays put, but doing it first is still better; it comes before L1.40 here only because T2-parse does. Touches: ast/ reads alternative numbers in 17 places. Candidates: Or, And, TypeAnd, TypeUnion, Path, Update, Suffix chains. Comparison chains `a == b == c` and else chains stay flat by their meaning. New notation for skip marks must be read by grammar.rs (one rule per line) and step L30.1's TextMate generator later. Grammar sync rule applies: edit prose, PEG and cases together, rerun the `check`, `cases` and `fuzz` tools, then `sync`. Item 5 of the TODO is an analysis first, so state the choice before rewriting. Conflict check: T6 may edit sec:grammar type rules; see T6's note. Concurrent DESIGN.md edits by ST3, T4 and T6 are in other sections.
- **T14-bugs-kernel.** T2-eval: rewrites eval.rs, so the two fixes go on the explicit-stack machine. *Note:* Both confirmed by running at f425b32, neither has a test yet, and neither is fixed on wt/t2eval (the Pow guard is unchanged there, eval.rs:1970). (1) eval.rs:750, the `Prim::Get` case on `V::Partial`: domain {0.0, 1.0} with a body reading f[-0.0] gave `{0.0 -> 5, 1.0 -> #notFound}`, while `Family::get` normalizes through `key()` and finds 0.0. Fix: `let k = &k.key()` before the defined, domain and busy lookups. eval.rs:789 `world.reads` has the same form (inferred; test it). (2) eval.rs:1385: 2**600000 gives Overflow while 2**300000*2**300000 succeeds with 600001 bits, so the result depends on how the value is computed. Fix: bound with (x.bits()-1)*e and let `sized` make the exact check. The tests go in `mod.rs`, not `scaling.rs`, which T2 edits, so this item needs no edge to T2 and stays early.
- **T2-parse.** LR1: adds the seed-growing loop to the parser's rule(). LR2: rewrites the AST builder, and makes long operator chains deep trees, which raises the depth this item must handle *Note:* Hard part: rule() and m() in parse/mod.rs are mutually recursive with a truncating kids list, so converting them means an explicit frame per Expr variant, including Not's save and restore of far and expected and the left-recursion loop, without slowing the packrat path. Reason for the edge: today `a+b+c` is a flat node; after LR2 a chain of n operators is n deep, so a recursive AST builder would overflow on long chains that work now. Precedes L1.40 by user order only; it shares no file with L1.40.
- **T5.** XE2: this question is in the round-2 brief and its answer picks the design. *Note:* Listed before L1.40 by user (item 5), but no technical need: shares no file with L1.40. I treat the later text as the rule; the DAG could relax it, and must in any case keep this before L1.41. - **T2-types.** T5, T6: both rewrite the search and BDD code in the same files, so converting first would be wasted work *Note (from T2-eval):* the operations on `Value` and `Family` in zed-kernel are iterative, tested at 1,000,000 deep on an 8 MiB stack (`crates/zed-kernel/src/deep.rs`, COMPLEXITY.md K22). COMPLEXITY.md K21 is left to T14-value: reading a variable deep-copies its value, so an accumulator is O(n²); `Items` is the one type that changes to shared parts. *Note:* Derived Clone, PartialEq, Debug and the compiler's Drop on the other deep types (`Term`, `Type`, syntax trees) are recursive too, so those values overflow even after the named passes are iterative. Each deep type needs a manual Drop and iterative compare, or shared interned nodes. This is the easy thing to miss in all T2 items.

Fix: a free list with a generation count for cells, and the same for `vals`; children of a constant read in place, with no push. `dec` allocates `vec![w]` on every call on the hottest runtime path, and it has no R row: return early for a non-cell and decrement in place when the count stays above 0. Also `c.count += 1` on u32 wraps in release after 2^32 shares: use checked_add with its own error. Pattern::Lit and Pattern::Is convert the whole word to a Value: test the tag first.

(files-light with T6, T11-fix-store and T2-mid in product.rs, and with T2-mid and T11-preimage in preimage.rs: merge resolves; run apart from them if possible.) *Note:* Confirmed by a scratch run against HEAD 644e333: the three arm results the step names already hold (x*x<100 gives [-9..=9], x*x<101 gives [-10..=10], x*y<100 leaves both unbounded). Remaining work: (1) forward transfer at product.rs:1591 treats the two operands of x*x as independent, so a binding z = x*x gets no square bound (inferred from the code, not run); (2) x*(x+1)<100 and y = x; x*y<100 stay unbounded, and only the alias case arguably falls under the step's words; (3) the random soundness test against the interpreter, a scaling test and negative controls. Spec gap: decide whether an alias through a let counts as 'one value by itself'; recommended yes, because the product already has each name's value. Also pending from xe-round2-orders, with no T number and the same file (product.rs): the Scope::narrow k^2 item and the two reduce-loop re-projection flaws, ordered before L1.40. T4: recorded compile behavior should be the fixed solver. T6: type printing may change (item 5), and ZIR text prints edge types, so snapshots taken earlier would all churn. No hand-written Rust baselines exist in the repository (a search of tests, tools and zed/compiler found none), so one Rust version per scalar L1 fence must be written, kept in the repository and built with --release. A place for them is unspecified; recommended tests/baselines/, excluded from the normal test run. Benchmarks stay under 60 s. Decide whether snapshots are canonical ZIR text or addresses: recommended text plus address, because text shows what moved. Any later change to lowering or to canonicalization (T11, T14 fixes) resnapshots; such a fix must carry its own snapshot review. DESIGN.md is edited only to record the numbers. Design: treat each field mentioned as a column whose values include 'absent', on the same search as tuples; this needs a cross-review round under the hard-algorithms rule and must meet the no-thresholds rule. The complexity rule and the quadratic-to-lookup rule require a better form (index arrows by universe and head constructor), or a stated reason beside the code; hence hard. The compiler must be in the running process; for a Zed-hosted compiler that is a linking question for zed-rt. Overlapping waves need two compiled copies live at once.

New crate not recommended: layout.md allows one only for a separate artifact or heavy native dependency. As specified, Zed's own syntax cannot be written as a struct grammar. Proposed fix: add `&` and `!` to GItem and either express HEAD by duplicating rules or add a parameter; alternatively name what stays host-level. Also fix whether the struct grammar reads bytes or the lexer's tokens.

- **PORTABLE-rt.** PERF-portable: the meters it lets run off Linux; and every end-of-layer test nothing else needs (L8.3 to L35.2), so that it is the last item. *Note:* User, 2026-11-04: portability to other OSes goes at the very end of the plan; AArch64 code generation stays where the plan puts it. It no longer feeds T12.
- **UNORD-keep.** L9.1: the reorder facet and the test this item extends. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from ARXIV-1208-spec (STUDIES3-spec). Feeds L9.2, L23.1, L27.2.
- **FOOTPRINT-peak.** No needs: M0.6 is done. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from ARXIV-1208-spec (STUDIES3-spec). Feeds L3.23, L24.5.
- **LAW-shape.** L3.8b: the moves class. L3.8: the obligation record, grades and rungs 1 and 7. L3.20: rung 6's bounded instances. L15.2: `law` declarations. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from ARXIV-2012-spec (STUDIES3-spec); the `==` and order classes (ARXIV-2012-2) are rejected, since they need exponentially many instances. Feeds L15.8, L15.9.
- **DESCENT-sum.** L6.12: the descent catalog the entry joins. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from STELLOGEN-spec (STUDIES3-spec). Feeds L17.1, L6.20.
- **ITEM-order.** No needs. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from STELLOGEN-spec (STUDIES3-spec). Feeds L3.8, whose obligation record the test compares.
- **TERM-id.** ADDR-stage: the `Part` key this item replaces. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 by STUDIES3-spec, from the ADDR-stage work. Feeds L1.40, T12.
- **TRACE-conway.** L6.9: the typing of `fix` (T-Fix evidence) whose identities it checks. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from TRACE-study: sec:categories says `fix` is a trace, a Conway operator on a cartesian category, and leaves open whether the seeded least fixed point over a carrier with failure atoms satisfies the Conway identities. Feeds L7.10a, which chooses between `fix` forms on the strength of its laws.
- **L6.11a.** L6.11: the loop-head widening whose landmarks it extends. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from MURAIL-study (A1): a contracting affine recurrence such as `y' = (3 * y) div 4 + x` otherwise widens to unbounded and becomes big-integer-backed. Feeds L6.20 and L31.4 (fixed-point filters on microcontrollers).
- **BEND-contract.** L6.5: the `.fold` over recursive unions whose layer plan this row is the alternative to. L5.7: the transition-table and semiring-matrix composition classes it reuses. L6.19: the costs of recursion that the planner weighs against the layer row. L3.14: the work-stealing scheduler that runs the heavy-path reductions. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from BEND-study (C1): a comb-shaped tree otherwise takes as many steps as it has nodes. Feeds L6.20, L24.1 (the strategy catalog records the choice) and BEND-gpufold.
- **BEND-gpufold.** L27.1: the GPU lowering and the `crates/zed-gpu/` crate it creates. BEND-contract: the contraction that runs as segmented scans. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from BEND-study (C2): grouping by height and constructor gives HVM2's `!` effect with no annotation and no per-lane recursion. Feeds L27.4.
- **TEST-fence-lines.** No needs. *Note:* Added 2026-11-03 from the ledger: the l01_07 snapshots failed after the BEND-spec and loop-boundary merges only because DESIGN lines moved. Feeds L1.41, as the ledger records; it changes the keys of the tests of L1.7 (done).
- **QUOT-classes.** L5.9: the union-find primitive that `classes` runs on. L2.11: the catalog entry it is written from. *Note:* Added 2026-11-04 from the study of arXiv:1503.03571 (Schultz and Wisnesky, *Algebraic Data Integration*; `wt/study-1503.03571/REPORT.md`, I1): Zed had `group` by a given classifier but no quotient by the equivalence that key pairs generate, so the DaoFP coequalizers row said "partly"; it now says "has", citing step L5.9a. The result depends only on the partition, never on the order of the links. The differential test needs no L6 step: the M0.9 kernel evaluator already runs a lattice `fix` (`crates/zed-kernel/src/eval.rs`, `FixKind::Lattice`). Union-find cannot split a class, so removing a link under incremental update recomputes its class; that site gets a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment. Feeds L5.14 and LIB-linkage.
- **LIB-linkage.** QUOT-classes: `classes` clusters the matched records. *Note:* Added 2026-11-04 from the study of arXiv:1503.03571 (I2): the sec:libraries row "dedupe, recordlinkage". Candidate pairs by a blocking key are a join, O(n log n). Reading and writing the records through stores (L21) comes with L33.1, which follows L21. Feeds L33.1.
